Root-caused from the operator's own run `logs/prints/print_20260727_171512_..._B6.jsonl`: PRINT_PATH streams each XY segment OPEN-LOOP (`move_xy_absolute` fire-and-forget, paced only by `time.sleep(seg_len/print_speed)`, nothing waits for arrival), but the Prior stage runs **~2.5× slower** than commanded (`SMS 11%`, measured `max=22282 µm/s`; optimistic SMS%↔speed cal + short ~0.48 mm accel-dominated segments), so the physical stage falls PROGRESSIVELY behind the stream — sampled `lag_um` grew **0 → 6144 (median) → 10736 (max) µm** across a ~10 mm print, end-of-path drain **timed out 30 s still 2.09 mm short** — while the pump extrudes on the fast software schedule → smeared pattern. Pickup/wash are point-to-point `safe_travel_to` (WAIT for arrival → slow≠wrong); PRINT_PATH is the only open-loop path (slow→wrong). **Two executor modes added to `_execute_print_path`, both gated by new `PrintSettings` flags (default False = legacy open-loop, so Full Print / Print Setup unchanged):** **(1) `velocity_follow` — CLOSED-LOOP velocity following, the Quick Print default (operator asked for continuous velocity correction, NOT stop-and-go):** NEW `_execute_print_path_velocity` runs a ~25 Hz (`_VEL_CONTROL_HZ`) loop that each tick polls the ACTUAL position (`get_xy_position(cached=False)`; poller already suspended for PRINT_PATH), projects it onto the toolpath → real **arc-length** progress `s` (`project_on_polyline`), deposits pump volume ∝ REAL `Δs` (`vol_per_mm=flow/print_speed`; non-blocking, no M400 in-loop), and commands a **velocity vector** (`send_velocity_xy`→Prior `VS,vx,vy` µm/s) toward a **carrot** placed `_VEL_LOOKAHEAD_MM` ahead of `s` (pure pursuit), ramping down over the last `_VEL_DECEL_MM`. Carrot tied to REAL progress ⇒ it can never run ahead of a slow stage (the open-loop failure mode) + position feedback re-centres each tick + pump tracks real distance ⇒ bead correct at any speed. SMS set to max + brisk accel so `VS` reaches the commanded velocity; velocity clamped to `safety_limits.max_xy_speed`. **Safety:** always stops the stage (`VS 0,0`) on every exit (abort/pause/ZP-drop/stale/runaway/completion/exception via `finally`); a sustained cross-track error (`>_VEL_MAX_CROSS_TRACK_MM` for `_VEL_RUNAWAY_TICKS` — e.g. a `VS` sign inversion) trips a **runaway guard** → `RuntimeError` → outer loop ERROR + retract to safe Z. Falls back to the discrete path on a stage without a continuous-velocity cmd (Ludl HLC). NEW pure helpers `polyline_arclength`/`point_at_arclength`/`project_on_polyline` (unit-tested). **(2) `confirm_each_segment` — stop-and-go fallback (landed first, NOT the Quick Print default):** waits for physical arrival (`_wait_for_xy_settle`, ±`_SEGMENT_SETTLE_TOL_UM`=40 µm) + drains the pump (M400) after EACH segment; correct but stop-start (slower), so superseded by velocity_follow for Quick Print, retained as a general executor fallback. **Also fixed:** the Timing-Calibration **"Run sweep" button was always greyed** — `_update_button_state` gates on live XY+ZP connection but was only called on construction / calibration-data pushes, never on `showEvent`/`on_status_update`, so a connect made after opening the page left it stuck; now refreshed on show + each tick. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (pattern correct/no smear + continuous motion; JSONL `path_start.mode:"velocity"` + `vel_sample.cross_um` stays small; **VERIFY `VS` DIRECTION FIRST on a low safe path — if the stage flies off, the runaway guard aborts+retracts → flip the `vx/vy` sign; tune `_VEL_LOOKAHEAD_MM`/`_VEL_CONTROL_HZ` to real latency**; sweep button enables once XY+ZP connected). Design: reused the existing `send_velocity_xy`→`move_stage_at_velocity`(`VS`, already used by Xbox jog) + poller-suspended PRINT_PATH; chose arc-length pure-pursuit over the existing time-based `VelocityExecutor`/`MotionController` (a time-parametrized target still runs ahead of a slow stage). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_quick_print_velocity_follow.py` (13, incl. integrating fake stage that follows `VS`/deposits-per-distance/runaway + spiral snap) + `tests/test_v75x_quick_print_confirmed_segments.py` (7); barrier regression (10); quick-print/timing/seam/pick-place/multi-ink/exec-log suites green. **Addendum (2026-07-27) — spiral projection-snap runaway FIXED:** first HW run tripped the runaway guard (`print_20260727_184045_..._C5.jsonl`) — NOT a sign error (tracking was ~100 µm for 6.5 mm, position responded in the commanded `VS` direction), but on the **spiral** toolpath `s` jumped **+4.8 mm while position moved 0.2 mm** then cross-track blew to 3.57 mm. Root cause: the projection's forward search window (`lookahead+4=4.6 mm` arc length) EXCEEDED an inner spiral loop's circumference, so the projection SNAPPED to the next loop (passes near the same x,y) → carrot teleported → runaway (+ a one-tick pump over-deposit). Fix: bound the projection search AND per-tick `s` advance to the physically-plausible `print_speed × real_dt × _VEL_SNAP_GUARD` (ceiling `_VEL_MAX_SNAP_MM`=1.5) — far below a loop's circumference (loops are a full revolution apart in arc length), well above the true per-tick advance, so `s` can't jump a loop and `Δs` (pump) is bounded. Locked by `test_bounded_window_prevents_loop_snap` + `test_completes_multiloop_spiral_without_snap`. Tuning note: on the innermost loops the fixed `_VEL_LOOKAHEAD_MM`=0.6 is a large fraction of the circumference → some corner rounding; reduce/adapt lookahead if inner loops print too round. **Addendum 2 (2026-07-27, "the old version worked on my other setup; why not here?"):** investigation (git + this machine's configs/logs) — the open-loop path is correct WHEN the stage tracks commanded speed; **this machine delivers only ~40% of commanded print speed and it's NOT a recent regression** — `print_timing_calibration.json` (2026-06-18) already recorded `5.0→1.85 mm/s` (correction 2.71), and today's open-loop log agrees (2.49→~1.0). Accel is fine (`SAS,80`, range [1,100]); Quick Print anchors speed% to the measured CRUISE top speed (22282 µm/s) the stage never reaches on a print's short segments → commanded ~2.5× > delivered → open-loop runs ahead → smear. The other machine tracks its rated speed / lacks the stored value. Decision (operator "do both, A/B on hardware"): Quick Print settings popout gains a **Motion mode** selector (`_motion_mode_combo`→`_motion_mode()`→`_build_settings` maps to the flags): **`open_loop` (DEFAULT — the original streamed path that worked on the other setup)**, `velocity`, `confirm` — so the same print runs each way on this machine for direct comparison; persisted with the profile. Tests updated (velocity 13→14; confirmed selector). Fastest fix for the old path on this machine: re-run Timing-Calibration "Measure top speed" (button works now) or lower the speed % until commanded ≈ real throughput. **Addendum 3 (2026-07-27, "the camera isn't a good tool for this — set up the calibrator a different way; my hunch is this stage is slower"):** the timing calibrator only used the camera as a still-vs-moving detector; the stage's OWN reported position is a better one (rest ≈0-1 µm/poll, motion ≈hundreds of µm/poll; immune to focus/texture/light). NEW `_EncoderMotion` (drop-in for `_FrameMotion` — same available/reset/metric; `metric()`=|Δposition| µm via `get_xy_position(cached=False)`, poller suspended during a run) + a **Detector selector** ("Stage position (no camera)" DEFAULT / "Microscope camera") → `_make_tracker()`; `_preflight` only requires the camera in camera mode. Both "Measure top speed" AND the settle sweep now run camera-free into the same store (`set_xy_max_speed_um_s`/`correction_for`) + `_apply_measured_xy_top_speed` fan-out — directly testing the "stage is slow" hunch (if measured top speed ≪ stored 22282 µm/s, storing it makes the mm/s↔SMS conversion real so a commanded print speed is achievable and the open-loop path stops running ahead). Follow-up lever (not wired; tried+reverted once — revisit with the camera-free measurement): pace open-loop `_execute_print_path` by the stored per-speed `correction_factor`. **Follow-up (2026-07-27) — settle sweep timed out on the encoder** (top speed worked; sweep's short/slow/streamed segments didn't): it reused the camera's scene-relative `_calibrate_threshold` — on a short segment the "peak" caught one poll spanning the whole ~260 µm move → threshold set high (~78 µm), but each sweep poll only advances ~40 µm (below threshold) → motion never detected → `_watch_until_still` timeout → no data. Fix: NEW `_encoder_threshold` (used by both `_run`+`_run_top_speed` when the tracker is `_EncoderMotion`) measures ONLY at-rest jitter (no move — the move-peak was the fragile part) and sets still-threshold `max(STILL_UM, floor×3, floor+3)`, far below real per-poll motion. Tests: `tests/test_v75x_timing_encoder_detector.py` (13) + timing suite (33 green). **Addendum 4 (2026-07-28) — comms-frequency checker + open-loop redefined as feed-forward velocity streaming.** (A) NEW `StageController.measure_control_loop_rate` (interleaved `send_velocity_xy`+`get_xy_position(cached=False)` cadence measured WHILE the stage oscillates ±amplitude, net ~0; poller suspended; always `VS 0,0`+return-to-start; speed clamped to `max_xy_speed`) = the real closed-loop `loop_period` that bounds the velocity follower's max stable speed — distinct from the poll-only `test_command_rate`. Persisted via `PrintTimingCalibrationStore.get/set_control_loop_ms` + `stable_velocity_speed_mm_s(lookahead,safety)` (`v≈lookahead/(loop·safety)`); surfaced as a **"Check comms rate"** button on the Timing-Calibration run row (XY-connected gate; retracts to Safe Z; no camera) logging loop ms/Hz/read/cmd + derived max stable speed. (B) Velocity-follower "back-and-forth over the same line" root-caused = **dead-time limit cycle**: the loop free-runs at serial-latency rate (~5–10 Hz, not 25), `VS` persists the whole tick, so when `speed×loop_period>lookahead` the stage overshoots the carrot → reverses → dithers, worst at the endpoint/reversals (15% decel floor > 40 µm arrive tol). Not a sign bug (that's a runaway). Dead-time-aware speed clamp is the proposed next fix; the checker measures the bound. (C) **Operator: open-loop should "just update the velocity vector needed, not move to prescribed points — that's confirm."** So `open_loop` → NEW `_execute_print_path_open_velocity`: feed-forward `VS` along the path **tangent**, target advancing by wall-clock time at commanded speed, NO position reads / NO point-to-point (continuous velocity for even laydown; `vol_per_mm=flow/print_speed` so `pace_correction` stretches time not volume; decel ramp + a final positioning move lands exactly on the endpoint; wall-cap + always-stop; no runaway guard — verify VS direction; falls back to the discrete point-stream with no `send_velocity_xy`). NEW helper `tangent_at_arclength`; `PrintSettings.velocity_open_loop`; Quick Print maps `open_loop`→`velocity_open_loop` (combo relabelled "Open-loop velocity (streamed vectors)"/"Confirmed per-segment (point-to-point)"); XY Challenge `_drive_open_loop` rewritten to the same streamer. `confirm`=point-to-point+wait, `velocity`=closed-loop pursuit (both unchanged). **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (comms Hz plausible; open-loop velocity lays a continuous bead — VERIFY VS direction on a low safe path first). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_comms_frequency_check.py` (12) + `tests/test_v75x_open_loop_velocity.py` (12); regression open-loop-velocity/comms/velocity-follow/confirmed/challenge/timing/encoder/barrier 104 + quick-print pick-place/multi-ink 61 green. **Addendum 5 (2026-07-28) — corner-aware closed-loop + shared control law + XY-Challenge rework** (operator: velocity closed-loop is the good mode; make it consume measured calibration, cut corner overshoot, rework the challenge bench, and fix confirm to stop only at corners). AskUserQuestion: scheduling **+ cross-track PID**; **both** ZN-relay + coordinate-descent auto-tune; configurable-small robustness matrix (PASS/FAIL vs a 30 µm resolution element). **NEW `SupportClasses/VelocityControl.py`** — the shared PURE law both the XY-Challenge bench AND `PrintManager._execute_print_path_velocity` run (tuning transfers): arc-length helpers MOVED here (PrintManager re-exports), `corner_flags`, `plan_speed_limits` (per-corner speed limit from turn angle, reachable ramp over `decel_mm`), `PursuitState`/`pursuit_step` (carrot pursuit + perpendicular **cross-track PID** kp/kd, clamped to `min(speed_cap, corner-limit)`), `resolve_control` (grounds control_hz in `control_loop_ms`, SMS/clamp in `xy_max_speed_um_s`, + a **dead-time speed cap** `v≤lookahead/((loop+phase)·safety)` that kills the pure-pursuit "back-and-forth" limit-cycle), `relay_ultimate_gain` (Åström Ku=4d/πa). Follower rewired onto it (measured-cal + corner slowdown + PID, all default-off = legacy; end-decel now ramps from the CAPPED speed → clean stop). **Confirm branch**: `corner_flags(points, confirm_corner_angle_deg)` → wait/drain ONLY at corners + last point, straight edges STREAM (barrier runs on streamed nodes) — so the star stops at its tips, not every node. New `PrintSettings` fields (default 0=legacy): `xy_max_speed_um_s`/`control_loop_ms`/`phase_lag_s`/`xy_accel_pct`/`vel_corner_angle_deg`/`vel_corner_speed_factor`/`vel_pid_kp`/`vel_pid_kd`/`confirm_corner_angle_deg`. Store: velocity/confirm defaults gain corner+PID keys; `get/set_resolution_element_um`(30), `get/set_robustness_matrix`, `get_phase_lag_s`. **`gui/dialogs/xy_challenge_dialog.py` reworked**: overlay recolour by role (ideal grey / newest **red** / best **green** / others low-alpha **blue**); per-mode param visibility (mode combo → `_refresh_param_visibility`); drivers call the shared law (bench==print); auto-tune = **coordinate descent** over each mode's ordered param list + a **Auto-tune PID (ZN)** relay button (velocity) → `compute_zn_pid_gains`; new **Robustness panel** (shape×size×speed matrix, PASS/FAIL vs the resolution element). Quick Print `_build_settings` stamps all new tuned + measured fields. Reused `MotionController.compute_zn_pid_gains` + `TrajectoryPlanner.detect_corners` logic. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (verify VS direction first; tighter corners + no back-and-forth; ZN/descent converge; confirm stops at star tips only; robustness green ≤ element). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_velocity_control.py` (17) + `tests/test_v75x_xy_challenge_panel.py` (8) + updated confirmed-segments (corner-only) + xy_challenge (new defaults); regression 125 + 124 + 32 green. |
| `MEBP_v75x_MOSAIC_SCALE_FOV_AND_REGISTRATION.md` | v7.5.x **mosaic/camera calibration batch** (operator, 2026-07-24: "the mosaic calibration is not working … it autocalculates the camera FOV but assumes a resolution — pull it from the camera hardware; the camera setup should auto-calibrate µm/px from stage motion; calculate the camera extents in microns by tracking edge features across a move; mirror the camera output in that view; a better registration algorithm — the current feature detection doesn't work when moving the stage; and the video everywhere should match the camera's aspect, not a fixed box"). AskUserQuestion: **stage-motion edge-feature auto-cal** (items 1-3) + **full pairwise + global-optimize** registration (item 5). Read-only agents root-caused each first. **(item 1 — "FOV assumes a resolution")** the mosaic FOV = `frame_w × _ploc_microscope_um_per_px(fw, um0)` = `objective_meas × cal_w` — pinned to the objective calibration's STORED resolution `cal_w`, which `ObjectiveCalibrationCard._on_calibrate_clicked` stamped from `config.camera_config.active_resolution` (stale/assumed). Fix: new `_true_capture_resolution(cam_idx)` reads the LIVE `frame.shape` (authoritative) → HW settings → config, used by BOTH the auto-cal and the existing objective cal, so µm/px is always stamped with the REAL captured resolution. **(items 2-3 — stage-motion scale + FOV auto-cal)** NEW `VisionDetector.select_trackable_patch` (most-textured central patch) + existing `find_template` (TM_CCOEFF_NORMED) track a feature across a LARGE baseline (where whole-frame phase corr loses lock); NEW `gui/dialogs/scale_fov_calibration_dialog.py::ScaleFovCalibrationDialog` (live feed + mirror control; auto-sizes the baseline to ~40% of the frame; QTimer state machine moves +X then +Y, tracks the feature each, derives `µm/px = move/|disp|` per axis, `FOV = frame_px × µm/px` extent, rotation via `plus_column_direction_deg`) — all anchored to the captured `frame.shape`, exposed as `result_um_per_px/_rotation_deg/_resolution/_fov_um`, >15% X-vs-Y disagreement warns; **"Auto-calibrate scale + FOV…"** button on `ObjectiveCalibrationCard` persists per-objective (`ObjectiveCalibrationStore.set_calibration`, true resolution) + live to the manager + rotation per identity — the SAME sinks the mosaic reads, so it flows into the mosaic FOV. **(item 4 — mirror in the calibration view)** `PixelCalibrationDialog` (and the new dialog) seed the feed with the camera's `(mirror, rotation)` via `set_view_orientation` + a **"Camera shows a mirrored image"** checkbox → flips the DISPLAY live, pushes `set_mirrored`, persists per identity; µm/px is measured on RAW frames so it can't corrupt the result. **(item 5 — better registration)** tiles were placed OPEN-LOOP by stage position with only ONE global median phase-corr shift on the display extent (weak confidence, no illumination/rotation handling); the richer batch path was gated off. NEW `MosaicBuilder._register_overlap_cv2` (CLAHE-normalise + Hanning + `cv2.phaseCorrelate` real cross-power peak as confidence; sign LOCKED by test) + `optimize_registration()` (registers EVERY overlapping pair over the retained canvas-res tiles → weighted global least-squares `_global_optimize_positions`, tile 0 anchored → `_reblend_at_positions` at the optimized positions; each correction BOUNDED to `_max_shift_px` so a bad match can't fling a tile; degenerate solve = no-op keeping the open-loop composite; bounded memory via the small canvas-res tiles, so the batch >200-tile gate is irrelevant). Wired in `calibration.py::_MosaicScanWorker`: full-plate scan builder now `retain_for_reorient=True`; after the tile loop → `optimize_registration()` → `finalize_global_shift` (extent offset, complementary) → detection on the improved composite → `free_reorient()` (frees tiles + ~190 MB accumulators, keeps the display cache). **(item 6 — camera aspect)** content was never distorted (`KeepAspectRatio` on the true `frame.shape`) — the operator saw LETTERBOX bars because the widget box didn't track the camera W:H; `CameraFeedView` gains `heightForWidth` (driven by `_last_image_size`, swapped for a ~90° view rotation) + a `setHeightForWidth(True)` size policy + `_maybe_update_geometry` (fires only on aspect change), so box/splitter hosts fill camera-shaped; grid hosts keep the undistorted fit (no regression). One base class → every live feed inherits it. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (auto-cal → plausible µm/px+FOV+rotation at the true resolution; scanned mosaic tiles line up better — flip the one-line `_register_overlap_cv2` sign if registration goes the wrong way; video fills its box with the camera aspect; mirror checkbox flips the feed + persists; FOV sizes from the real resolution). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_camera_scale_fov_and_registration.py` (11, incl. the +7 px injected-error optimize/sign-lock); mosaic (58) + objective/rotation/mapping (77) + camera-store/correction (49) + cal-liveview/hw-controls (32) suites green. **Addendum (2026-07-24, operator follow-ups):** **(A)** the mosaic "still had the wrong values" because a stale **"Store FOV/spacing" learned value in `MosaicAlignmentStore` SHADOWED** the fresh objective/scale calibration (precedence: learned→else objective, and un-rescaled) — fixed: a fresh calibration now CLEARS the learned value (`ObjectiveCalibrationCard._clear_stale_mosaic_fov` + `_mosaic_align_key` matching `_ploc_camera_objective_key`) and the mosaic rescales BOTH the learned value (by its stored resolution) and the objective fallback (via `CameraManager.effective_um_per_px`) to the scan frame width. **(B)** new **"🔍 Verify with a quick mosaic"** button on `ScaleFovCalibrationDialog` builds a 3×3 self-check mosaic at the current position with the measured µm/px (reuses `_MosaicScanWorker`+`MosaicBuilder`+global-optimize, applies the camera orientation, displays the composite) — **safe: only moves XY at the current height, never descends** (retract Z=current, `target_z=None`). **(C)** new opt-in **`CameraFeedView(auto_orient=True)`** re-reads the camera's saved mirror/rotation from `CameraManager.view_orientation` each frame (display-only + click-inverting; edge-pick-suppressed), enabled in both calibration dialogs so the calibration view stays consistent with the live/mosaic views (scoped to the dialogs — `CameraManager` has no role getter, and a blanket default would wrongly rotate the ~45° needle feeds; enable per-view on any workflow microscope feed still found inconsistent). Also fixed a pre-existing gap: `test_v75x_plate_mosaic._FakeCtrl.safe_travel_to` lacked the `apply_insert_floor` kwarg the earlier rosette-Z work added. Tests: suite now 19; regression 94 + 90 green. **Addendum 2 (2026-07-24, 2nd follow-up):** **(1)** the "Microscope Camera Setup" block's resolution went stale when the camera **settings gear** changed the device resolution (only the block's own combo drove the device, not the reverse) — fixed with a signal chain `CameraSettingsDialog.resolution_applied(cam_idx,w,h)` → `CameraFeedView.resolution_changed` → `HardwareSetupPage._on_slot_resolution_changed` (updates `camera_config.active_resolution` + the block combo for the microscope slot). **(2)** the mosaic's x,y-vs-camera mapping was flipped (camera-axis-vs-stage orientation wrong) — corrected via the operator flip controls in (3), which persist per identity + propagate. **(3)** the Scale/FOV "Verify" is now **"🔍 Verify & correct (mosaic)…"** which opens the full `MosaicCalibrationDialog` (reused) seeded with the measured µm/px + orientation + current pos + no-descent safe Z + the objective keys — giving the X/Y spacing sliders + rotate + Store-FOV + **relabelled flip buttons "⇄ Flip X axis" / "⇅ Flip Y axis"**; new `MosaicCalibrationDialog._propagate_um_per_px` (from "Store FOV/spacing") pushes the corrected µm/px to the live manager (click mapping) AND the objective store (mosaic fallback + restore) so a correction is GROUND TRUTH everywhere; the card clears the stale learned FOV BEFORE the dialog (not after) so a fresh correction survives; Scale/FOV re-syncs its result from the manager after the correction so Accept can't clobber it. Tests: suite now 26; regression 78 + 112 + 52 green. **Addendum 3 (2026-07-24, 3rd follow-up):** **(1)** the full-plate scan "didn't apply the calibrated rotations/scales" — wiring existed (`_ploc_microscope_frame_orientation` + `eff_um_per_px`), so hardened: orientation now falls back to the persisted `CameraCalibrationStore` value when the live manager has none, and the scan logs `Mosaic scan applying: resolution … µm/px … FOV … rotation … mirror … registration …`. **(2)** "registration is very bad — explore better methods (Fourier-Mellin) + a manual backup": NEW `MosaicBuilder._register_overlap_fourier_mellin` (log-polar of the FFT magnitude → rotation+scale via phase corr, then de-rotate/scale + phase-corr for translation; CLAHE; recovers −9,−4 / −8°/×0.909 on synthetic data), used by DEFAULT in `optimize_registration(method=…)` with phase-corr fallback + median rotation/scale diagnostic; **manual backup** = a `registration_method` param + a "Registration method" combo (Fourier-Mellin / Phase / **Off — manual/stage only**), where Off makes optimize a no-op and the operator aligns by hand. **(3)** camera resolution now has ONE source of truth — `camera_config.active_resolution` ALWAYS drives the microscope on start (`_on_camera_started_hw` no longer gates on hw_controls; `_apply_hw_controls` skips resolution for the microscope; `_maybe_apply_resolution_to_device` reads active_resolution) so the camera reads the setup block as ground truth. Tests: suite now 34; updated `test_active_resolution_is_single_source_for_microscope` (active_resolution wins over hw_controls — intended contract change) + settings round-trip for `reg_method`; regression 63 + 115 green. **Addendum 4 (2026-07-24, 4th follow-up — "I literally just calibrated it"):** **(A)** the mosaic's objective-confirm warned "⚠ No stored per-objective calibration" although the µm/px in use WAS the calibrated value — KEY MISMATCH: `_ploc_confirm_objective` looked up the objective store via `_get_camera_model_for_idx()` (extracted model TOKEN) while the card WRITES + the FOV read (`_ploc_microscope_um_per_px`) use the full `camera_spec.name`; fixed to use `camera_spec.name` (the calibration was stored + used; only the note was wrong). **(B)** "full mosaic not using the correct flip/rotation": `_ploc_microscope_frame_orientation` read the LIVE manager, which can come up UN-SYNCED after navigating from Hardware Setup → saw 0°/unmirrored; fixed to per-field precedence preferring the PERSISTED `CameraCalibrationStore` ground truth (manager fills absent fields) + a diagnostic log of the resolved rotation/mirror + source. Tests: suite 36; regression 83 + 64 green. **Addendum 5 (2026-07-24, "the mosaic is not flipping the x axis"):** the correction dialog's flip/rotate (`MosaicCalibrationDialog._orient_set`) pushed orientation LIVE to the manager but persisted to the store ONLY on "Apply to camera setup" — so a flip made without Apply never reached the store, and the full scan (which reads the store as ground truth per Addendum 4) saw no mirror. Fix: `_orient_set` now PERSISTS IMMEDIATELY (`_persist_orientation` → `CameraCalibrationStore.set_rotation`/`set_mirrored` per identity) on every flip/rotate, so the flip is ground truth at once and the full mosaic applies it via `_orient_tile` at tile placement. Tests: suite 38 (flip persists mirror, rotate persists rotation); regression 65 green. If the WRONG axis flips, it's an `_orient_tile` sign issue, not persistence. **Addendum 6 (2026-07-24, per-camera Flip X/Y + rotation):** (A) fixed the objective-confirm KEY MISMATCH (`_ploc_confirm_objective` used `_get_camera_model_for_idx` model-token vs the card's full `camera_spec.name`) → the false "No stored calibration" warning. (B) operator: "on each camera a checkbox to flip axis X, Y + custom rotation" → AskUserQuestion: ONE unified system affecting live view + mosaic + click-mapping, per-slot on Hardware Setup → Cameras. Added an INDEPENDENT **flip Y** to the existing (mirror=flip X, rotation) so the transform is `R(θ)·diag(sx,sy)` in `MosaicBuilder._orient_tile`, `CameraFeedView._orient_qimage`, and `pixel_to_stage_offset` (negates dy); `CameraManager.get/set_flip_y`+`full_orientation`; `CameraCalibrationStore.get/set_flip_y`; `_ploc_microscope_frame_orientation→(rotation,flip_x,flip_y)` (store=ground truth) threaded into both scan builders + feed; per-slot **⇄ Flip X axis**/**⇅ Flip Y axis** checkboxes + **Rotation °** spin (push-all-consumers + persist per identity + restore); the calibrate dialog's flips toggle flip_x/flip_y independently. Tests: scale-fov suite 44 + rotation-monitor 42 (per-slot push/persist/restore); regression 90 + 91 + 48 green. **Addendum 7 (2026-07-27, "the placement of the images is not on the correct side … stage +X ≠ camera +X, same for Y"):** operator had to manually flip X+Y to make the objective mosaic stitch, got it **upside down**, and the full mosaic placed tiles **on the wrong side**. Root cause: `_orient_tile` was fed the operator's **eyeball live-view flips**, but a **mirror is not a rotation** and `plus_column_direction_deg` **explicitly assumes non-mirrored**, so the scale/FOV auto-cal derived rotation ONLY and **discarded the handedness** — even though the +X and +Y move displacements (`_disp_x`/`_disp_y`) encode the mirror in their **cross-product sign**. Combined with the plate view's `plate_flip_180`=True (default from `None`) 180° display flip, it double-counted. **Fix (measure, don't guess):** new `scale_fov_calibration_dialog.derive_camera_stage_orientation(dxX,dyX,dxY,dyY)` decomposes the FULL camera→stage 2×2 (`M=-(d/µmpx)·P⁻¹`) into the canonical `(rotation_deg,flip_x,flip_y)` that `_orient_tile`/`pixel_to_stage_offset` (`R(θ)·diag(mx,my)`) consume (`sign(det)=mx·my` → handedness on flip_y; θ from M's col0), **sign-locked** by `test_derive_camera_stage_orientation` (identity / mirror-X→(180°,flipY) / mirror-Y / **180° mount→(180°,NO flip)** / 90° / 90°+mirror) and provably == `plus_column_direction_deg` for any non-mirrored camera (no regression). `_compute` stores `result_flip_x`/`result_flip_y`; the caller (`ObjectiveCalibrationCard._on_autocal_scale_fov_clicked`) pushes them to the manager (`set_mirrored`/`set_flip_y`) + persists per identity alongside rotation → the live view, mosaic `_orient_tile`, and clicks all use the ONE **measured** orientation. Verify-step re-sync reads `full_orientation`. **Also WYSIWYG:** `MosaicRegistrationView.set_flip_180` (whole-view 180° rotation) + `MosaicCalibrationDialog(plate_flip_180=None)` auto-resolves from `controller.plate_flip_180()` → the calibration view renders in the SAME plate frame as the full mosaic (no more "upside down here, wrong side there"). Manual flips remain as override; if the GLOBAL sign is inverted on a given camera build (documented `phaseCorrelate`/`plus_column_direction_deg` caveat) one manual flip corrects + persists. Tests: scale-fov +10 (`TestDeriveCameraStageOrientation`×8 + `TestScaleFovComputeDerivesFlips`×2) + mosaic-adjust +5; regression 145 green. **Addendum 7b (2026-07-27, "calibration builds correctly the first try, but the full-plate mosaic scan still isn't correct"):** DECISIVE root cause of the full-plate divergence = a **double orientation**. The full-plate `_MosaicScanWorker` applied a **legacy coarse per-tile `_orient_frame`** (`none/rot180/fliph/flipv` from `mosaic_scan.frame_orient`) to each raw frame, THEN the builder applied the **calibrated `_orient_tile`** (rotation+flipX+flipY from the store) on top → orientation TWICE. The operator's `settings.json` had a stale `frame_orient="rot180"` (a pre-calibration compensator), so the whole-plate scan was rotated an extra 180°. The **calibration dialog worked** only because it's built with `settings={}` → `frame_orient` defaults `"none"` → single orientation (`_orient_tile`) — exactly "calibration right, full plate wrong." **Fix:** RETIRE the coarse `frame_orient` in `_MosaicScanWorker` (`self._frame_orient` forced `"none"`; `_orient_frame` now an identity no-op; the `frame = self._orient_frame(frame)` call removed; the scan setup no longer reads/passes it) → tile orientation is applied ONCE by the calibrated `_orient_tile` on both the full-plate scan and the calibration-dialog worker (same class). `settings.json`'s stale `rot180` left as-is — the SEPARATE fluorescence worker (`_SingleWellMosaicWorker`, unoriented builder) still reads that shared key for its own coarse orientation (documented follow-up); the plate mosaic now ignores it. Test: `test_worker_does_not_double_orient`; plate-mosaic/fluorescence/mapping/scale-fov/mosaic-adjust regression 121 green. Combined with Addendum 7 (measured handedness), the full mosaic now assembles the same as the calibration. |
| `MEBP_v75x_SKETCH_OVERLAP_AND_START_END_MARKERS.md` | v7.5.x **Print Builder → Sketch: closure OVERLAP + draggable start/end markers** (operator: "some shapes don't close due to the printing method; add a start↔end overlap — a **distance** overlap or a **needle-diameter** overlap that continues along the shape's own path past the start; also control start + end by **dragging markers along the path**"). AskUserQuestion: closed-shape end marker = **overlap handle** (slides past the seam, synced with the numeric control); open shapes = start+end **trim** to a sub-segment. **Model (one source of truth):** RETIRES the boolean `SketchShape.overlap_closure` → `overlap_mode` ("none"/"needle"/"distance") + `overlap_distance_mm`, and adds `end_point` (OPEN-shape trim end). `overlap_amount_mm(od,bead)`: needle = **one full needle OUTER Ø** (operator said "diameter"; the retired bool used the RADIUS — fallback one bead) / distance = typed mm / none = 0. Conditional-emit → **legacy sketches byte-identical**; `from_dict` MIGRATES legacy `overlap_closure:true` → `overlap_mode:"needle"`. **Compiler/geometry:** new arc-length helpers `_cumlen`/`_project_arclen`/`_point_at_arclen`/`_subpath_arclen` + **`trim_open_path(path,start,end)`** (projects both anchors onto the polyline, returns the sub-path; end unset → far extreme → reduces EXACTLY to the legacy flip, byte-identical). `_shape_paths`: closed unfilled → `reorder_path_to_start` (seam, unchanged); open unfilled with a start/end anchor → `trim_open_path`. Over-closure overshoot is now **per-shape** `shape.overlap_amount_mm(_od,bead)` fed to the existing `extend_closed_path` (closed unfilled only), in both `compile_to_trajectory` and `plan_print_sections`. `_resolve_start_on_shape` for open shapes now returns the nearest point ALONG the path (not just an endpoint) so markers trim anywhere. **Canvas:** new **red ■ end marker** (mirrors the green ▸ start flag) — OPEN shapes always (trim end, default = far endpoint); CLOSED loops only once `overlap_mode != none` (the overlap handle at seam + overlap arclen on the rolled ring via `_closed_ring`). Drag (`_apply_resize` `"end"`): open → `end_point`; closed → project cursor onto the ring → `overlap_mode="distance"` + `overlap_distance_mm = arclen-from-seam`. `set_needle_od` (page feeds the outer Ø so a needle-mode marker matches the compiler); `clear_end_point`; `_shape_supports_end`/`_effective_end_world`/`_end_marker_screen`/`_end_handle_at`. (Fixed: canvas had no `numpy` import — `_closed_ring` uses the arrays `_shape_paths` already returns.) **Page:** closed loops get a **Closure overlap** combo (None / Needle Ø / Custom distance) + distance spin (Custom seeds a visible default so the handle appears off the seam); open shapes get a **Print end** custom/default row + "Reset end point" (mirrors the start row). **Needs GUI verification on ME3B V1.** Tests: `tests/test_v75x_sketch_overlap_start_end_markers.py` (25) + migrated `..._overlap_sequence_needle_mode` (33); all 19 sketch suites green (327) + print-library/quick-print-multi-ink/travel-split/sketch-edit/extrusion-volume (77). |
| `MEBP_v75x_SKETCH_PARAMETRIC_CONSTRAINTS.md` | v7.5.x **Print Builder → Sketch gains parametric-modeling constraints** (operator: "tangent, join, coincident, parallel, horizontal, vertical, concentric"; chose via AskUserQuestion the **full set** — + perpendicular, equal length/radius, driven **distance/radius dimensions**, fix/lock — with **live solve while dragging** and **snap→constraint auto-capture**). **Architecture mirrors `PlateSketchSolver`** (scipy `least_squares` LM/TRF, residual rows per kind, transient weight-1000 `drag_ghost`, DOF via Jacobian rank) but operates **directly on SketchShape DOFs** (line/polygon = vertices; circle = cx,cy,**r**; ellipse/rect = center+size; travel = cx,cy; `region` excluded) — no entity-graph restructure. **Only shapes referenced by ≥1 constraint enter the variable vector** (pay-for-play; unconstrained sketches never touch the solver). **Stable ids:** new `SketchShape.id` (0=unassigned, emitted only when ≠0) + `Sketch.constraints`/`SketchConstraint{id,kind,refs=[[shape_id,anchor]…],value,mode,weight}` (emitted only when non-empty) → **legacy sketches serialize byte-identically** (regression-locked); ids assigned lazily by `ensure_shape_ids()` on first constraint use. Index refs impossible — `optimize_print_order` REORDERS the list (verified it never mutates vertex order, so `p{k}` anchors stay valid); `backtrace_shape` copies clear `id` (dup-id corruption); `prune_constraints()` drops refs to deleted shapes/stale vertices (canvas delete cascades). Anchors: `center`/`p{k}`/rect corners `c0..c3`/`shape` + virtual drag anchors `mid`(line)/`centroid`(polygon). Tangent = line↔circle (cross²/len²−r²) or circle↔circle with `mode` external/internal **chosen from the drawn geometry** (`tangent_mode_for`); `equal_radius` is a plain residual (radius IS a variable here, unlike the plate solver); `fix` excludes the shape's DOFs (a locked shape's drag ghost is structurally filtered → dragging it moves NOTHING). Ghost held **solver-side** (never on the model → an undo snapshot mid-drag can't serialize it); structural `_constraint_valid` filter makes predicted rows EXACT (conflict attribution + LM/TRF choice). **NEW `SupportClasses/SketchConstraintSolver.py`** (reuses `SolveReport`/`DOFStatus`); write-back clamps sizes ≥0.05 mm. **Canvas:** constrained move/endpoint drags → ghost pin + 33 ms-throttled `QTimer` solves (`_begin_cdrag_ghost`/`_queue_cdrag`/`_flush_cdrag`/`_end_cdrag`); size-handle drags → "plain" throttled re-solve; group ops + typed edits → `solve_after_edit()`; `_snap` now records provenance (`_snap_hit`; `_snap_vertices` yields `(x,y,idx,anchor)`) so **drawing/dragging onto a vertex auto-captures coincident, onto a line/circle edge → point_on** (`_maybe_add_snap_constraint`, deduped, toggleable), wired into line/circle/rect-corner/polygon commits + endpoint-drag release; `add_constraint_for_selection(kind)` = selection-based creation (Join auto-resolves the NEAREST anchor pair; point-on resolves curve-vs-point with a line+circle convention: circle=curve; fix toggles); constraint **glyph layer** (H/V/∥/⊥/=/T/🔒 pills, coincident rings, dashed distance leaders + `↔ value` labels, conflicts render RED from the last report); `constraints_changed` signal; undo/redo round-trips constraints (they live in the sketch dict). **Page:** persistent **"Constraints" card** (DOF/status line: fully-constrained / N DOF free / ⚠ conflicting; "Capture joins while drawing" checkbox; 13 per-selection-gated buttons; constraint rows = click-to-select-shapes label + editable value spin for dimensions + ✕ delete, rebuilt on the debounced preview tick with a focus guard so typing in a value spin isn't yanked). Constraints are **design-time only** — compiler/welds/optimizer semantics untouched. **Needs GUI verification on ME3B V1.** Tests: `tests/test_v75x_sketch_constraints.py` (43); all 18 sketch suites green (300) + print-library/quick-print-multi-ink/travel-split (44). |
| `MEBP_v75x_MOSAIC_ORIENTATION_ADJUST_TOOL.md` | v7.5.x **interactive "Fix orientation" tool on the plate mosaic → saves the camera's mirror + rotation** (operator, 2026-07-24: "for any mosaic we build … buttons that flip/rotate the individual images to match the correct view … these settings then applied to the camera setup … continuously change and set the actuals"). AskUserQuestion: correct **feed + mosaic**; **flip H/V + 90° + fine angle**; **instant re-blend from retained tiles**. **Reworks the two prior same-day mirror cuts** (source-flip; rotation downstream) into a clean **raw-frames + apply-per-consumer** model so the mosaic can re-blend against a *changing* orientation: (1) **display** — `CameraFeedView.set_view_orientation(mirrored, rotation_deg)` transforms the SHOWN pixmap (flip+rotate, matching `_orient_tile`) so the feed reads upright/un-mirrored, and **inverts clicks back to raw frame coords** via `QImage.trueMatrix().inverted()` (so `pixel_to_stage_offset` is unaffected); overlays mapped through the same xform; opt-in per view, no-op at (False,0°), suppressed in `set_edge_pick_mode` (needle row=Z). (2) **click→stage** — `pixel_to_stage_offset` re-applies mirror (`dx→−dx`) then `R(θ)` on the raw click. (3) **mosaic** — `MosaicBuilder._orient_tile` mirror+`R(θ)` per tile, **retains the small canvas-res tiles** (`retain_for_reorient`) + `set_frame_orientation`/`reblend_reoriented` (zero accumulators + re-blend every retained tile; `_accumulate_oriented_tile` factored out; `free_accumulators` a no-op while retaining, `free_reorient` releases). **camera_widget** source-flip reverted → `_mirrored` is a stored FLAG only, frames raw (`_grab_frame`/`capture_fresh_frame` return raw); `camera_manager.get_rotation_deg`/`get_mirrored`/`_widget` getattr-safe (clears the pre-existing `test_v75x_spheroid_picker_scaling` `_rotation_deg` errors) + `view_orientation` convenience. **calibration.py:** `_ploc_microscope_frame_orientation()`→`(rot,mir)` fed into the full-plate + well-scan builders (so the mosaic renders oriented); live feed corrected via `_ploc_apply_feed_orientation` (`_ploc_ensure_live_camera` + `_refresh_ploc_view`). **[RELOCATED per operator: the flip/rotate tool lives in the single-well "Calibrate mosaic scan" dialog, NOT the full-plate Plate Location mosaic — it's fast + already has the manual slide/image-gaps registration.]** `MosaicBuilder.tile_images_px` now applies `_orient_tile` per tile (no-op default) so the dialog's per-image `MosaicRegistrationView` reflects the orientation. **`gui/dialogs/mosaic_calibration_dialog.py`** seeds `(mir,rot)` from `CameraManager.view_orientation`, builds with it, keeps the builder alive (retained frames), and adds a **"Camera orientation (flip / rotate images)"** group (`⇄ Flip H`/`⇅ Flip V`/`⟲ 90°`/`⟳ 90°`/Rotation-° spin/**Apply to camera setup**) — each control re-orients the shown tiles (`set_frame_orientation`+`_show_tiles`) + pushes the camera live (feed+clicks+future scans), Apply persists to `CameraCalibrationStore` (flip-H=`rot→−rot`+toggle-mirror, flip-V=`rot→180−rot`+toggle, rotate=`±90`, normalised). `retain_for_reorient`/`reblend_reoriented`/`free_reorient` remain a general (unused-in-prod) builder capability. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (scan → flip/rotate until correct + feed upright → Apply → re-scan correct; clicks land right; restart restores; needle edge-pick + rotation cal still work; flip an `_orient_tile`/`_orient_qimage` sign if a control goes the wrong way). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_mosaic_orientation_adjust.py` (12: builder retain/reblend/identity/free + feed-view transform/click-inverse/90°-dims/edge-pick-suppress) + updated `camera_rotation_cal_and_monitor` (mirror back in mapping; widget flag-only; `view_orientation`) + `mapping_camera_orient_and_rosette_z` (builder mirror back); camera store/liveview/correction/hw-controls/picker-scaling + mosaic/reanchor/fluorescence/plate-location-page suites green (236). |
| `MEBP_v75x_DARK_PALETTE_WHITE_BACKGROUND_FIX.md` | v7.5.x **fix — "multiple pages have a white background now"** (operator, 2026-07-24). Root cause (investigation): the dark theme is a **QSS only** (`styles.py::build_theme` via `MainWindow.setStyleSheet`), but **no dark `QPalette` is set anywhere** (`main.py` does `app.setStyle("Fusion")` but never `app.setPalette`; repo-wide grep = zero `setPalette`), and the QSS has **no `QScrollArea`/`QGraphicsView` rules** + no base-`QWidget` background. Fusion follows the **OS/system palette** when none is set, so the palette `Base` role is white on a light-mode OS (or after a Qt dark-mode-detection shift) — and **every widget the QSS doesn't explicitly paint renders white**: QScrollArea viewports (most page bodies), QGraphicsView viewports (5 view classes — `_CalibrationPlateView`/`_CalibrationYZView`/`WellPlateView`/`ProjectionPane`/`ProjectionView` — set **no** background brush, unlike 5 siblings that do `setBackgroundBrush(COLORS["base"])`), and the fully-transparent left context panel (`#contextScrollArea QWidget { transparent }`) that bottoms out at `Base`. **Not from the recent camera/mapping work** — `styles.py`/`main.py`/calibration styling all unchanged (git); an external OS/Qt palette shift exposed the latent fragility. **Fix:** new `main.py::_apply_dark_palette(app)` (after `setStyle(fusion)`) pins a Catppuccin-Mocha `QPalette` (`Base #181825` = the viewport bg, `Window #1e1e2e`, `Text #cdd6f4`, `Button #313244`, `Highlight #cba6f7`, disabled `#6c7086`, PlaceholderText guarded for Qt-version) so the dark theme is decoupled from the OS theme — standard practice for a Fusion+dark-QSS app. The QSS still overrides per-widget wherever it sets an explicit bg (styled widgets unchanged); only the previously-white uncovered viewports/panels now default to dark, across **all** pages incl. needle calibration. Verified headlessly (`Base==#181825`, `Window==#1e1e2e` after apply); tests make their own `QApplication` (no palette) so unaffected. **Needs operator visual confirmation on the running app.** Optional follow-up: add `setBackgroundBrush` to the 5 brush-less QGraphicsViews for palette-independent consistency (the palette fix already darkens them). |
| `MEBP_v75x_MAPPING_CAMERA_ORIENTATION_AND_ROSETTE_Z_CLAMP.md` | v7.5.x two operator reports (2026-07-24, follow-up to the per-camera rotation/mirror work), each root-caused with a read-only investigation agent first. **(1) Camera µm/px + mirror + rotation must be applied when mapping the plate/rosette.** Finding: the live-feed click paths (manual click-rim, re-anchor, click-to-move) already route through `CameraManager.pixel_to_stage_offset` (µm/px + rotation + mirror) — but the **mosaic BUILD** placed raw frames (`MosaicBuilder._blend_tile_to_composite` did only a `cv2.resize`, no per-frame rotation/mirror — only a coarse manual none/rot180/flip dropdown that ignored the calibration store), and every mosaic back-projection (`MosaicWellMappingDialog._on_confirm`, `_ploc_fit_from_mosaic_detections`, `MosaicWellRemap`, rosette sub-well offsets) is a raw `stage = extent + px/scale` map (µm/px only). So tile CENTERS were right but within-tile offsets back-projected the wrong way for a rotated/mirrored camera (mirror flips handedness; error grows to ~½ FOV). **Fix at BUILD time** (the only place per-tile orientation about each tile's own centre is available): `MosaicBuilder` gains `frame_rotation_deg` + `_orient_tile(tile)` (`R(θ)` about the tile centre, matching `pixel_to_stage_offset`; **no-op fast path** at 0° = byte-identical legacy placement; applied after the resize in `_blend_tile_to_composite`, so incremental + rebuild + all scan paths inherit it). Then the composite is stage-aligned and the existing raw back-projection is correct for free (all four consumers unchanged). `CalibrationPage._ploc_microscope_frame_orientation()` reads the microscope slot's `get_rotation_deg` and feeds the full/rosette scan + `_well_scan_builder`. Large non-axis rotations clip tile corners (mapping stays correct, only peripheral coverage lost — overlap covers it). **Rotation sign needs real-HW verification** (mis-signed θ rotates the mosaic the wrong way — same caveat as the rotation calibration). **MIRROR is NOT handled in the builder** — a mirrored camera is un-mirrored at the frame source (`CameraWidget`, see `MEBP_v75x_CAMERA_ROTATION_CAL_AND_MONITOR_CAMERA.md`), so the builder already receives an un-mirrored image; the initial cut passed `frame_mirrored` here + flipped in `_orient_tile`, removed when the mirror moved to the source. Fluorescence mosaic + mosaic-calibration-dialog builders left unoriented (documented follow-up). **(2) Rosette mapping clamped Z instead of going to the assigned safe Z.** Root cause: the rosette scan/map retracts pass `_safe_z` to `safe_travel_to`/`ensure_retracted_to`, but those apply the **insert-clearance floor** `_min_travel_z_mm` (raise to clear the tallest tube), armed **only when `plate.max_rim_height_mm > 0`** = rosette tube inserts (exactly why plate mapping was unaffected). Two facets: (a) the floor overrode the assigned safe Z during imaging-height mapping travel where it isn't needed (needle never descends there, `target_z_mm=None`), and (b) `app.py::_update_insert_clearance` computed `top_z + max_rim + margin` **without the `z_up_sign` factor** — the same polarity bug already fixed in `PrintTrajectoryPlanner._well_print_z`. Fix: new `apply_insert_floor: bool = True` on `safe_travel_to`/`ensure_retracted_to` (False skips the floor → exact assigned safe Z), passed False by the 3 calibration mapping/scan retracts (pre-scan `ensure_retracted_to`, `_MosaicScanWorker` first-tile, `_AutoReanchorWorker`); **print/pick-place travel keep the floor (default True)**; + `_update_insert_clearance` now uses `top_z + z_up_sign * (max_rim + margin)` (no-op on the current `z_up_sign=+1` ME3B V1 profile, correct on -1). **Safety:** the floor is bypassed ONLY for imaging-height mapping travel (needle stays at the assigned safe Z = imaging height, inherently above the tubes, never descends), so it cannot drag through a tube; well-descending travel keeps the floor. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (mapped well/sub-well centres land correctly with a calibrated rotated/mirrored camera; rosette map retracts to the assigned safe Z with no clamp; printing over a rosette still clears the tubes). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_mapping_camera_orient_and_rosette_z.py` (13) + mosaic (`test_v731_mosaic`/`_mosaic_memory_and_overlay_perf`/`_mosaic_orientation_remap`, 44) + z-retract/jog-travel/plate-location-z (36) green; no-op default preserves all existing mosaic + floor behavior. |
| `MEBP_v75x_CAMERA_ROTATION_CAL_AND_MONITOR_CAMERA.md` | v7.5.x three operator requests (2026-07-24). **(1) Per-camera rotation-vs-stage calibration on Hardware Setup → Cameras:** every slot mini-card (Camera Detection & Assignment) gains a row-3 strip — a role-aware readout `Rotation vs stage: θ° (Δ from nominal) — <mount hint>` + a **"⟳ Rotation…"** button (enabled while that camera runs) that launches the existing stage-motion `PixelCalibrationDialog` and commits **ROTATION ONLY** (`result_rotation_deg` → `CameraManager.set_rotation_deg` live + `CameraCalibrationStore.set_rotation` per device identity, µm/px siblings preserved — the needle/objective µm/px calibrations keep their own flows). Nominal mounts per role: **microscope** views along Z → rotation is in the stage **XY plane** (axis-aligned nominals 0/±90/180°); **needle X/Y** side cams mounted **~45° to the stage X/Y axes** (±45/±135°); monitor axis-aligned. New pure helpers `role_nominal_rotations`/`nominal_rotation_delta`/`role_rotation_hint` (module-level in `hardware_setup.py`; Δ is a sanity hint only — motion always uses θ itself via the existing `pixel_to_stage_offset` path). When the calibrated slot holds the MICROSCOPE role the fresh rotation also syncs into every per-objective calibration via new public `ObjectiveCalibrationCard.adopt_camera_rotation` (extracted from `_on_calibrate_orientation_clicked`, which now reuses it) — the stale-per-objective-rotation guard from `MEBP_v75x_REANCHOR_MOSAIC_AND_CAMERA_ORIENTATION.md`. Readouts restore on slot assignment through the existing `_restore_calibration_for_slot` rotation restore; refresh hooks in `_refresh_camera_preview_state` + `_refresh_role_derived_displays`. **(2) New "Monitor" camera** — rests on the stage, overviews the entire operation: `CameraRole.MONITOR` (singleton, "Monitor (overview)" in the role combo, info badge) + **`MAX_LIVE_CAMERAS` 3 → 4** (4th slot card; `CameraManager` default + `app.py` now source the constant instead of a hardcoded 3; `from_dict` pads legacy 3-entry `camera_roles` → migration-free). **(3) Per-camera "Mirrored view" flag** (follow-up): a **"⇄ Mirrored view"** checkbox (row 4 of each slot card, enabled once the slot has a source) that says the camera shows a left↔right flipped view. A mirror reverses image **handedness** — which a proper rotation can NEVER express — so it's a separate flag. **The feed is flipped at the FRAME SOURCE** — `CameraWidget._maybe_mirror_frame` (shared by `_grab_frame` *before the raw cache* and the direct `capture_fresh_frame` mosaic path) `cv2.flip(frame, 1)` when set — so **what the operator SEES is un-mirrored** AND the cached raw frame used by detection / mosaic / click-mapping is the same corrected image; nothing downstream re-mirrors. New `CameraCalibrationStore.get/set_mirrored` (a `mirrored` bool sibling of `rotation_deg`; stored only when True, False pops the key → legacy entries byte-identical, µm/px/rotation/image-correction siblings preserved) + `CameraWidget._mirrored`/`set_mirrored`/`mirrored` + `CameraManager.get/set_mirrored` **delegate to the widget** (like image correction; local `_mirrored[]` cache for no-widget slots) + `_on_toggle_mirror`/`_apply_slot_mirror` (push live + persist per identity) + restore in `_restore_calibration_for_slot` + "· mirrored view" appended to the rotation readout. Geometric flip (before the raw cache), unlike brightness/gamma (display-only, after). Mirror lives ONLY in the identity store (not the objective store — survives objective swaps since the objective push never touches `set_mirrored`). **[UPDATED 2026-07-24 — operator: "if i select mirror camera it needs to mirror the output of the camera feed so what i see is not mirrored." The initial cut applied the mirror as a click→stage flip in `pixel_to_stage_offset` + mosaic `_orient_tile` with the display left un-mirrored; reworked to flip at the source (above) so the DISPLAY is corrected and the mapping/mosaic drop their mirror terms — `pixel_to_stage_offset` and the mosaic builder now apply rotation only.]** **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1/V3** (calibrate each camera's rotation → plausible angles ≈ nominal; toggle Mirrored view on a flipped feed → live-view clicks drive the stage the right way; 4th Monitor slot assigns/starts/previews; readouts + mirror restore after restart). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_camera_rotation_cal_and_monitor.py` (35: 22 rotation/monitor + 13 mirror) + camera-store/liveview/rotation/objective/role/reanchor/correction/hw-controls/async-open/fluorescence/hardware-page suites green (one hard-coded 3-slot expectation in `test_v744_calibration_revision` made `MAX_LIVE_CAMERAS`-agnostic). Pre-existing unrelated: `test_v75x_spheroid_picker_scaling` 5 errors (`CameraManager.__new__` fake lacking `_rotation_deg`, from concurrent working-tree WIP — confirmed with this change stashed). |
| `MEBP_v75x_STARTUP_CAMERA_FREEZE_AND_CONTEXT_PANEL_WIDTH.md` | v7.5.x two startup-usability fixes (operator: "the camera boot-up freezes the UI" + "the left context menu can't be moved out fully so I can't see many of the buttons; it goes away if I stretch the window wider"). **(1) Camera freeze:** the OpenCV *probe* was already backgrounded (`detect_cameras_async`) but the *device open* still ran on the GUI thread — `_start_saved_cameras` (startup auto-start of saved cameras) → `CameraManager.start` → `CameraWidget.start()` → the multi-second `cv2.VideoCapture(idx, CAP_DSHOW)` / `ToupCamBackend.open` / `AndorBackend.open` — freezing the event loop (and every `QTimer` camera feed) until each opened. **Fix:** new `CameraWidget.start_async(on_done=)` opens on a daemon thread and marshals the opened handle back to the GUI thread via a new private `_open_result` signal → `_on_open_result` assigns it + starts the display `QTimer` there; `_release_handle`; `_open_token`/`_opening`/`_pending_on_done` state; `stop()` bumps `_open_token` so an in-flight open is **superseded** (result discarded + handle released → a stop/restart mid-open can't leave a camera streaming). Simulated/already-running/unavailable finish synchronously. New `CameraManager.start_async` routes to it, emitting `camera_started` from the completion callback (same signal contract); **`CameraManager.start` kept SYNCHRONOUS** because the mosaic-scan path grabs a frame immediately after start. `_start_saved_cameras` now uses `start_async`. Enumeration (DirectShow/ToupCam/Andor) left on the GUI thread (COM prefers the main thread; it's the fast part). **(2) Context panel width:** the left box had `minimumWidth = s(340)` and a dynamic max `total − reserve` with `_content_reserve_px = s(460)`; on any window narrower than ~`min+reserve` (×1.5 at 150 % DPI) the max dropped below the min, so the panel opened at 340 (< its designed 540) and clipped the buttons, and couldn't be dragged. **First fix (`app.py`):** content pane (slot 1) made **non-collapsible** (yields by shrinking/scrolling, no "snap to full open"); `_content_reserve_px` reduced 460→240. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1/V3** (UI responsive while saved cameras come up + all feeds stream). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_camera_async_open_and_context_width.py` (8: async open success/fail/supersede-on-stop, manager `start_async` emits when running, sync `start` intact, bounds never-below-min/grows) + updated `test_v75x_camera_calibration_store.py` (stub `start_async`); camera/context regression 141 green. **Addendum (2026-07-24) — responsive "sized to fit" content (operator: "content of the left context menu … sized to fit … adaptive text/button sizing, almost like a css file for html" → then "now it looks terrible … jog axis sections should be aligned … sized to fit the width … no scrolling … its ok to scrunch the buttons, we just need to make sure the text gets smaller as well"):** a first FlowLayout-wrap attempt looked ragged → **removed** (`flow_layout.py` deleted). Final = **aligned grid, fit-to-width, no horizontal scroll**. New **`gui/widgets/responsive.py`** (`container_scale(width,design,lo,hi)` = CSS `clamp()` on top of DPI `scale_factor()`; `quantize`). `jog_button_array.py` rewritten: the 3 step rows (XY/Z/P) share **ONE `QGridLayout`** (col 0 label · cols 1-5 magnitude buttons · col 6 custom) so labels + button columns align across rows; magnitude buttons/custom/pump columns are `Expanding` w/ equal column stretch → always fill+fit the width (scrunch when narrow); direction pad = centred cluster. font-size/min-width pulled out of the buttons' QSS so `resizeEvent`→`container_scale(width,_DESIGN_BASE=470,0.62,1.15)`→`_apply_scale` rescales every label/button/field point-size + height + label-col width + pad-button size (**text shrinks with the width**, centralised `_BASE_*`/`_FONT_*`); **public API + jog signal contract unchanged** (verified + rendered offscreen at 260/380/540 px). `app.py` context-box min `s(540)`→**`s(260)`** (still opens at `LEFT_BOX_WIDTH`); `context_panel_host` native `QScrollArea` gets `ScrollBarAlwaysOff` (horizontal). Vertical scroll stays (full panel taller than most windows — eliminating it needs collapsible sections/tabs, flagged for operator). Nav sidebar left as-is (labels fit its 200 px expanded width). **Rev 2 (operator: "min width should be 100px and each item should take up a %% of the width"):** made the WHOLE panel proportional + fit to 100 px — box min `s(260)`→**`s(100)`**; jog buttons/fields/pumps keep equal column stretch with tiny minimums (stretch drives width, font floor lowered to 4.5 pt so text keeps shrinking); speed rows / position rows / Absolute-Go-To / Hardware-Info made proportional (`Ignored` h-policy + tiny min + stretch), section headings `setWordWrap`, Refresh + safety-warning shrinkable; `Card` title uses `Ignored`+tiny-min so a long title elides not floors; and `JogButtonArray` / `HardwareControlPanel` / `StandardJogContextPanel` each override `minimumSizeHint()` to a small width so the scroll area can drive them to 100 px (natural min 338→~96). Verified offscreen through a real `QScrollArea` host at 100/130/200/320 px — fully proportional, no horizontal scroll. **Rev 3 (operator: "why can't the items just get smaller by a %% of the width?"; AskUserQuestion → scale text, not a QGraphicsView zoom):** Rev 2 scaled the jog buttons' fonts but let the other sections *clip* their text; Rev 3 makes it uniform — new `responsive.scale_descendant_fonts(root, factor, skip_subtrees=)` (records each widget's base pt once, sets `pointSizeF = base×factor`), called from `HardwareControlPanel.resizeEvent` + `StandardJogContextPanel.resizeEvent` with `factor=container_scale(width, s(440), 0.45, 1.12)` (control panel skips the self-scaling `JogButtonArray`; context panel skips the self-scaling `HardwareControlPanel`). Stripped hard-coded `font-size` from the resolved-speed label + Hardware-Info rows' QSS so they scale (QSS font-size beats setFont); the verbose safety-warning banner now scales down too instead of dominating. Verified offscreen at 140/240/340 px (`_font_factor` 0.44→0.54→0.78, all text scales together, no clipping). Tests: `test_v75x_responsive_context_panel.py` (15, +`TestDescendantFontScaling`) green at 1.0× and 1.5×. |
| `MEBP_v75x_PUMP_JOG_OFF_GUI_THREAD.md` | v7.5.x fix — "when I use the 3D printer board to move the pump, the cameras stop displaying frames." Root cause: `HardwareControlPanel._on_jog_pump` (the shared pump-jog handler behind the "Jog" pill panel used everywhere, and Hardware Setup) called `StageController.move_pump_uL(...)` synchronously on the GUI thread; with `device_profile.backlash_comp_enabled: true` (set on ME3B V3), that brackets the click with a TAKE-UP → MAIN → UNLOAD sequence, each sub-move **blocking** via an M400 drain + settle dwell — up to several seconds on the Qt event loop, during which no `QTimer`-driven camera feed (`camera_widget.py`) can tick, so the live view appears frozen. Same class of bug as `MEBP_v75x_JOG_TRAVEL_OFF_GUI_THREAD.md`. **Fix:** `_on_jog_pump` now runs the actual `move_pump_uL`/`move_pump_relative` call on a daemon thread (mirrors the existing `SafeTravelWorker` / `calibration.py::_compcal_step_move` idiom) with a busy-guard (overlapping clicks ignored, not queued) and a queued `_pump_jog_done` Signal back to the GUI thread for the post-move position refresh. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V3** (jog a pump with backlash comp on while watching a live camera feed — no freeze). Tests: updated `test_v75x_axis_max_speed_inputs.py` / `test_v75x_common_axis_speed_source.py` (wait for the backgrounded move before asserting); 62 tests green; pump-compliance/plunger-setup/jog-direction suites green (1 pre-existing unrelated failure, confirmed present with this change stashed). |
| `MEBP_v75x_PRIOR_III_BAUD_AND_MULTI_BAUD_DETECT.md` | v7.5.x fix — "the Prior ProScan III is connected for sure, but the software says it can't find it" (ME3B V3, after swapping the Ludl MAC 5000 back out for a Prior III). **Two independent root causes, both found via live read-only diagnostics:** (1) baud mismatch — `proscan_iii.json` assumes 38400, this unit (`H117P1N4/F` via `STAGE`) was sitting at 9600 (Prior's manual: no DIP switch for baud exists — confirmed absent from the full manual; it's a firmware/EEPROM setting that **reverts to 9600 if the port sits idle across 2 power cycles**, the likely cause); (2) this unit's `V` (firmware_version) query returns a bare `R` instead of a version string (confirmed at both 9600 and 38400 — a genuine firmware behavior, not a baud artifact), so `proscan_iii.json`'s V-based detection could never match it regardless of baud. **Hardware fix (live, 2026-07-23):** researched + confirmed Prior's own documented `BAUD,<code>` command (`96`/`19`/`38`/`115` → 9600/19200/38400/115200, acks `0`) against Prior's official ProScan II/III manuals; stepped the unit 9600→38400→115200 (max, Rev-F/USB-FTDI per the `/F` suffix), verifying communication after each step. **Software:** new `config/controllers/proscan_iii_h117.json` (same Prior III wire syntax, verified identical `format_command` output to `proscan_iii.json`) with `STAGE`-based detection (`identify_tokens: ["STAGE =","MICROSTEPS"]`, `wake_delay_ms: 300` — 300ms alone truncated the multi-line reply, ~600ms settle needed) and `communication.baud_rates: [115200,38400,9600]`; new **`ControllerProtocol.baud_rate_candidates`** (absent ⇒ `[baud_rate]`, byte-identical for every existing protocol) + `XYStage._find_with_protocol` now retries every candidate baud per port — resilience against the exact fail-safe reversion that caused this, not just a one-time fix. Named after the stage model (`_h117`), not the baud, since the baud changed twice mid-session. **Also found+fixed in the same investigation:** the Device-page XY-controller combo (`_on_xy_controller_changed`) persisted to `settings.json` but never called `_persist_active_profile()` (unlike "Save XY Calibration"), so the on-disk device profile stayed stale and a later profile Load would silently revert a fresh controller choice — fixed there and in the equivalent gap in `settings_page.py`. **A mistake made and recovered during this session:** an errant `Settings()` call without `.load()` wiped `settings.json` to near-defaults (`device_profile.active`, calibration section, etc.) — recovered cleanly via `git restore --source=HEAD -- settings.json` (the file was tracked before `.gitignore` added it) per the operator's own suggestion; verified byte-identical to HEAD apart from the one intended field. All settings/profile writes now use the load-modify-save pattern, never a bare constructor. Tests: `test_v75x_prior_multi_baud_detect.py` (8) + full Ludl/jog/speed/axis-map regression (114) green; real-HW verified end-to-end through `XYStageManager` (detected, connected at 115200, live position + speed/accel readback all correct). |
| `MEBP_v75x_LUDL_MAC5000_XY_CONTROLLER.md` | v7.5.x **new XY-stage controller: Ludl LEP MAC 5000** alongside Prior ProScan II/III (operator: "we have a new type of xy stage … using a LEP MAC 5000 controller"; ensure correct commands + consistent speed/accel/travel per stage). The XY layer was a JSON-protocol abstraction but **5 behaviors were hardcoded Prior-isms** in `XYStage.py`; this generalizes them **protocol-driven, defaulting to Prior so `proscan_ii/iii.json` are byte-for-byte unchanged** (a regression test freezes that). **Decisions (AskUserQuestion):** per-machine + auto-detect · research + tunable · build a sim. **`ControllerProtocol`** gains optional getters (all Prior-safe defaults): `family` (`prior`/`ludl`), `command_mode_init_bytes` (from `command_mode_init_hex`, e.g. Ludl `FF41` = raw `0xFF 0x41` sent at connect to force ASCII/high-level mode), `ack_success_token`+`match_ack_error` (Prior bare `R` vs Ludl `:A`/`:N -<code>`), `get_position_parse` (Prior `x,y,z` CSV+strip-`R` vs Ludl `WHERE X Y`→`:A <x> <y>` whitespace/2-axis), `speed_model`/`speed_units`/`speed_is_per_axis`, `accel_model`, `position_scale` (stage wire-units per µm, Prior 1.0 / Ludl ≈10 counts/µm). **`XYStage`** applies the scale on move send + inverse on parse (`_position_scale`), `_drain_ack` (Prior `R` drain / Ludl `:A`/`:N` recognize, non-fatal), `_parse_position_response` now a protocol-dispatched **instance** method, `set_speed_mm_s`/`set_velocity` branch on `speed_model` (`_set_speed_absolute` → per-axis `SPEED` counts/s, clamped to the µm/s ceiling AND `speed_range` for safety), serial open reads `stop_bits` (Ludl 2) + writes the init bytes, `__init__` sim branch picks `LudlStageSimulator` when `family=="ludl"`. **Per-machine selection:** `DeviceProfile.xy_controller_json` (+`xy_max_speed_um_s`) round-trips and writes into the SAME global `controller.controller_json` key on apply (precedence device profile → global → auto-detect; None = inherit); `StageController.set_controller_json` applies on the next XY connect (no restart); Hardware Setup → Device "XY controller" combo + accel range/label relabel per model (Prior 1-100 / Ludl 1-255 ramp); settings-page dropdown pushes it too. **New `config/controllers/mac5000.json`** (researched from Micro-Manager `Ludl.cpp` + shared ASI/LEP command set: `MOVE`/`MOVREL`/`WHERE`/`HERE`/`SPEED`/`ACCEL`/`HALT`/`VER`, `:A`/`:N` acks, CR term, 9600-8-N-2, counts) with bench-TBD defaults flagged (µm/count, SPEED units pulses/s-vs-mm/s, stop bits). **New `SupportClasses/LudlStageSimulator.py`** (standalone — zero regression to the Prior sim; µm internally, counts on the wire, `:A` acks, own `sim_xy_state_ludl.json`). Ludl HLC has **no continuous vector-jog** (`set_velocity` null) — rather than leave Xbox-stick jog a dead no-op, `move_stage_at_velocity` detects the missing command and delegates to new `XYStage._jog_pulse` (integrates the commanded velocity over wall-clock time into an accumulator, fires a real `move_stage_relative`/`MOVREL` once the delta clears one wire count; resets on stick-release) — click/point-to-point jog was already unaffected. Travel envelope stays µm-native (`SafetyLimits` clamps in µm ABOVE `XYStage`; scale only inside → `plate_axis_sign`/`plate_flip_180` unaffected). **Real-HW verified 2026-07-22 on COM5** (an FTDI USB-serial, confirmed 9600-8-N-2 + `0xFF 0x41` init; `VER`→`Version no. : 5.100 MAC5000 Interface with USB`; `WHERE X Y`→`:A 399 321`; detection fixed to probe with `WHERE X Y` not `VER`, since the real VER reply isn't `:A`-prefixed; a live `MOVREL` executed the exact commanded count delta) — **and the connect-time port scan was fixed to never OPEN the ZP/Marlin's COM port** (`XYStageManager(exclude_ports=…)`, fed from `StageController.zp_connected_port`/`_preferred_zp_port`) since merely opening a serial port asserts DTR and auto-resets an Arduino/Marlin board. Still needs a live-Xbox bench check of the pulsed jog's feel, plus `xy_position_scale`/`speed_units` bench-tuning against a measured distance. **VE/VECTOR (Prior-style continuous-velocity) tested LIVE and confirmed unsupported** (`:N -1` — real hardware, zero-motion probe), settling the "can it move at velocity" question definitively: no, this firmware has no such primitive; the pulsed fallback is the correct approach. **ACCEL bench-CONFIRMED backwards from typical intuition** — it's a ramp-TIME index, LOWER is FASTER (a 200 µm move: 0.61s at ACCEL=1 vs 0.88s at ACCEL=255, same SPEED) — `parameters.acceleration` default flipped 100→1, UI label/hint updated, a freshly-selected Ludl profile now defaults the spinbox to 1 instead of inheriting Prior's mid-range 50. **New live-readback capability** (motivated by a real incident: a malformed `SPEED X=0.3` corrupted only X's SPEED register to 84 counts/sec while Y stayed at 230400, with zero app-visible sign of the divergence) — `get_max_speed`/`get_acceleration` protocol commands (Prior bare `SMS`/`SAS` query; Ludl per-axis `SPEED {axis}?`/`ACCEL {axis}?`), `XYStage._query_protocol_value` (write+read, unlike the fire-and-forget `_send_protocol_command`) + `get_speed_readback`/`get_acceleration_readback` (tolerate the real Ludl reply quirk of trailing junk on single-axis queries, e.g. `:A 20 N-2`), and a new "Live Stage State" card on Hardware Setup → Device (Read-from-stage button, per-axis X/Y display, yellow mismatch flag for Ludl, collapses to one shared row for Prior) — verified against the real hardware read-only. Tests: `test_v75x_ludl_protocol_semantics.py`/`_ludl_xystage.py`/`_ludl_simulator.py`/`_ludl_xy_prior_regression.py` (60, incl. `TestExcludePorts`/`TestJogPulseFallback`/`TestReadback`) + jog/axis/speed/z-retract/z-setup (146) + calibration/orientation (58) green. |
| `MEBP_v75x_ILLUMINATION_LED_CONTROL.md` | v7.5.x new **dimmable microscope illumination LED** (ZF-R25-0403 12 V COB) driven off the ZP board's **FAN0** output, brightness set from the app (operator). **No axis / no device-profile change** — the LED is not motion; it rides a spare fan PWM pin. **Backend:** new `ZPStageManager.set_led_brightness(level)` (clamps 0-255, sends **`M106 P0 S<v>`** via the existing `ok`-handshaked `send_data`) + guarded `StageController.set_led_brightness` passthrough (no-op/False when `is_zp_connected` False). **No firmware flash for the default path** — FAN0 (`PC6`) is already `M106`-controllable in stock SKR Mini E3 V3.0 Marlin; flash `FAST_PWM_FAN` only if bench testing bands the camera (M106 vs M355 share the pin timer — command choice doesn't fix flicker). **Bed-heater output rejected** (its slow PWM would band the imaging camera; ≤ 1 A fits the fan MOSFET). **GUI:** one reusable `gui/widgets/illumination_control.py::IlluminationControl` (on/off `QCheckBox` + 0-100 % `QSlider` + live label; ~100 ms debounce so drags don't flood the shared serial bus; every write guarded on `is_zp_connected`; `on_status_update` greys on disconnect; silent `set_on`/`set_value` for state restore) — embedded as a `Card("Illumination")` in `StandardJogContextPanel` (rides the left-box **"Jog" pill** everywhere that panel appears) **and** registered as a drop-in custom-panel module `register_section("illumination", … "💡 Illumination LED")` reusing the same widget (no host/panel/store change). **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V1** (LED lights/dims/off; no camera banding at real exposures; card greys when ZP disconnected; ZP `ok` handshake stays healthy under slider spam). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_illumination_led.py` (16); context-panel/zp-serial/zp-position-override/jog-pump-fill suites green (66) + offscreen jog-panel smoke. |
| `MEBP_v75x_CALIBRATION_USABILITY_POPUP.md` | v7.5.x new **"usability" pop-up communicating when XY / Z / P (pump) were last calibrated + recalibration warnings** (operator, changes.txt L22 — Cason 7.21). New per-machine **`SupportClasses/CalibrationStatusStore.py`** (`config/hardware/calibration_status.json`; `$MEBP_CALIBRATION_STATUS_DIR` override) owns a lifetime **XY travel odometer** (µm), per-type **`calibrated_at`** timestamps (xy/z/p; xy snapshots the odometer so travel-since-cal derives), and the two **thresholds** — `xy_recal_travel_mm` (**M**) + `recal_interval_hours` (**H**) — living here (not `HardwareConfig`) since only the pop-up reads them. **Odometer** = `PositionPoller.on_xy_travel`/`_accumulate_xy_travel` fires each poll-sample chord (catches ALL motion — jog + programmatic) → `StageController._note_xy_travel_um` → store (thread-safe + throttled ~15 s + `flush()` on shutdown); guards: `_last_odom_xy` reset on `set_stages`, jitter floor 1 µm, 1 m frame-discontinuity cap. **Timestamps** stamped where each calibration commits: **P** in `stage_panel._pump_setup_persist`, **Z** in `stage_panel._z_setup_capture_top` (apply_z_setup), **XY** in `calibration.py::_save_calibration` (change-signature gated so a Z-only save OR a calibration *load* — `_xy_cal_loaded` seed — doesn't bump XY). **UI:** "Recalibration Reminders" group (M/H spins + Save + **"Calibration status…"** button) on Hardware Setup → Device (`stage_panel._build_recal_reminders_group`). **Pop-up:** `gui/dialogs/calibration_status_dialog.py` (`evaluate_calibration_status` pure + `show_calibration_status(parent, force=)`); `app.py` startup trigger (`_maybe_show_calibration_status`, once/session, only-when-attention — clean setup never nags; `force=True` button always shows) + `save_settings` odometer flush. **Decisions (AskUserQuestion):** startup-only-when-needed + always-available button; ALL stage motion counts. **Needs real-HW/GUI verification on ME3B V1.** Tests: `tests/test_v75x_calibration_usability_popup.py` (14) + offscreen panel/dialog smoke; last-known-calibration/jog-nav green (63). |
| `MEBP_v75x_ROSETTE_INK_PICKUP_WELL.md` | v7.5.x **fix — on a rosette plate the Quick Print ink pickup drove to the rosette PARENT centre instead of the assigned ink sub-well** (operator: "the needle goes to the center of the rosette not the ink rosette well"; buffer pickup was correct). **Root cause:** `ink_locations` (`ink→list[well]`) is **append-only** (`HardwareConfig.assign_wells_to_ink`, no plate pruning), so an ink pinned to `"A2"` *before* A2 became a rosette lingered as a stale index-`[0]` entry and adding the real sub-well gave `"Inert GFP": ["A2","A2.a"]`. `PlateDesign.compile()` **drops** the rosette parent (compiled plate has `A2.a/b/c`, no bare `"A2"`), but calibration re-adds the parent at the sub-well **centroid** in the `well_positions` map — and every reagent-pickup resolver took **`wells[0]`** = `"A2"` → dipped at the centroid `(87234,67824)`. Buffer worked only because its list was one real sub-well. (The Well-Setup *seed* path already validated vs `plate.well_names`, which excludes parents; the pickup resolvers validated vs `well_positions`, which includes the centroid — the asymmetry.) **Fix (all reagents + self-clean, operator-chosen):** new `_reagent_prep.resolve_pickup_well(wells, plate)` prefers a real leaf well in `plate.well_names` over a flattened parent (falls back to `[0]` → byte-identical legacy); `service_well_names`/`resolve_service_positions` gain a `plate=` param; applied at all pickup sites (`quick_print._ink_source_well` + multi-ink, `cell_targeting`/`cell_labeling._reagent_source_well`, spheroid's private `_service_well_names`) with `self._plate` threaded through every service call. **Data hygiene:** `HardwareConfig._drop_redundant_parents` (drop bare `"A2"` when `"A2.<x>"` is in the same list — plate-independent string rule) in `assign_wells_to_ink` + `_prune_ink_locations` (one-time `from_dict` migration `["A2","A2.a"]→["A2.a"]`); new `clear_all_ink_locations()`. **UI:** new **"Clear all wells"** button (confirmed `_loc_clear_all`) in Hardware Setup → Ink → Reagent Locations = a hard fresh-start reset that wipes stale/hidden entries. **Needs real-HW verification on ME3B V3** (ink aspirate lands on `A2.a`, not the centroid). Tests: `tests/test_v75x_rosette_ink_pickup_well.py` (16) + quick-print-pick-place/rosette-flatten/plate-types/cell-targeting/cell-labeling/spheroid/workflow-settings/ink-location-assignments+colors/multi-ink suites green (289). |

Name-keyed store paths → a future "Bright Field 6" needs only a `CHANNELS`/`CHANNEL_NUMBERS` entry. **Needs GUI verification on ME3B V1.** Tests: `tests/test_v75x_fluorescence_mosaic.py` (35, +2) + common-print-settings/workflow-settings-popout/pump-settle-prime suites green (92). |
